Let's understand the LP method in state of art: "Starting from nonnegative f normalized sum sqrt(2n), set M=||f*f||∞. Compute g0 by LP maximizing sum b subject to b_j≥0 and ||f*g0||∞≤M. Could be more effective than subgradient because it explicitly finds a feasible g under current peak bound M. If current M is feasible for f itself (g=f gives max M), LP maximizes sum, preserving normalization. It may find a better g with same bound relative to f, then blending f and g can reduce max. 6. Optimizer details. We can improve FISTA with:
- Exact evaluation of maxconv and track active lags.
- Use a "top-k + LSE" hybrid gradient. At high beta, LSE approximates max but may focus too many lags. Top-k exact subgradient can escape.
- Use line search/backtracking on smooth LSE to choose step. FISTA step =1/L where L β? For LSE, Lipschitz constant ~4β sum? We can use trust/LS? Simpler: step = α / (β * ||a||_2^2?) Previous step=scale/beta. We can tune.
- Use gradient normalization to keep progress: step = η * S / ||grad||? For nonconvex, maybe use constant small step with restarts.
- Use "smoothed max" with adaptive beta and occasional exact subgradient.
- Use periodic "noise/kicks" to escape local min: add random perturbation projected to simplex, reset momentum. The kick size proportional to gap between current best and a target? Could use simulated annealing: if no improvement for T iterations, kick larger.
- Use "multi-mode": run FISTA from a candidate, every time stuck, perturb the current best with random noise and continue.

Maybe use "alternating direction" with two candidates: best and current. If current not improving, keep best and restart from best with different hyperparams/noise. Keep track.

7. Active-set polishing: When FISTA gets very close, use exact subgradient with top active lags and a decreasing step. But to improve global, use finite differences? Maybe use coordinate descent on active lags.

Can also implement a "box-and-project" active-set LP approximate: Given current a and M=current max, find a direction d in null? We can solve a small quadratic to reduce active conv: choose d that decreases all active c_k equally while preserving sum and nonnegative. This is a linear system: For active set A, want ∇c_k(d) = -δ for k∈A, maybe 1^T d=0. Since ∇c_k/2 = P_k a? Wait gradient of c_k at a is 2P_k a. If we move a += t d, c_k changes ≈2 t (P_k a) d? Actually c_k(a+d)=c_k(a)+2a^TP_k d + d^TP_k d. Linear term 2(P_k a)·d? P_k a is vector v with v_i=a_{k-i}. So we want choose d such that v_k·d negative for active k. This is a linear feasibility: minimize max_k v_k·d subject to sum d=0, d>=-a. Since v_k are shifts of a. Could solve via subgradient. FISTA does similar.

Second-order term d^TP_k d can be used: if we choose d negative on pairs for high lags, quadratic may increase. But small.

Maybe use "projected gradient with greedy lag descent": For the most active lag k, gradient is 2P_k a. Projected gradient descent reduces it. Multiple active lags average.

8. Could use quadratic programming to find d that minimizes maximum active linear term under L2 norm (a trust region). This is convex (if trust region) and can be solved by scipy.optimize.minimize? For n dimension large, but active set small. We can restrict d to a low-dimensional subspace (e.g., top Fourier modes or coordinate subspace around active pairs). But maybe overkill.

- Beta starts low (5) to explore landscape, increases to high (500-2000) to polish.
- Step scale maybe 0.5-2.
- For each candidate, run multiple FISTA instances in a loop, with time slices 10-30s. Use random seeds and beta rates.
- Use "local chasing": Start from best sequence plus noise σ increasing (0.0005,0.001,0.005,0.01,0.05 relative to S/sqrt(n)) to escape. Evaluate after FISTA.

But too many random restarts may waste time. We can structure:

Phase C (35%): Intensive polish on best at n=8192/16384 with active/exact, many beta values, noise kicks.
Phase D (15%): Larger n=32768 if time: upsample best, optimize; also possibly downsample; final.
This is similar to previous but with multi-start and parametric.

FISTA optimizer:
Inputs: init array a (nonnegative), time_budget, rng, maybe target base.
We need choose objective normalization. Let S = sqrt(2n). Then objective = max c (since 2n c / S^2 = c). We'll track best_maxconv.
Normalize initial to S: if sum>0, a=S*a/sum; else uniform.
Initialize y=a, t=1.
Beta schedule: `beta = min(beta_max, beta_init * exp(beta_rate * it))`.
Modes:
- 'lse': grad=lse_gradient(y,c,beta)
- 'topk': grad=topk_gradient(y,c,k,power)
- 'hybrid': mostly lse; when stuck use exact_subgradient/topk.
But we can improve by using gradient of the actual objective: If we use LSE approximation with beta, the smooth objective is (1/β)LSE(β c). Its gradient = lse_gradient. The true max gradient is exact subgradient. We can combine: grad = lse_gradient(beta) but when beta high, it approximates active. For exact subgradient, step size should be ∼1/L where L~? Use adaptive.

We can use backtracking line search on smooth objective value to ensure descent? That requires extra conv? We already compute objective each iteration. FISTA with fixed step 1/L can stall if L overestimated. We can use a step sequence: `step = step_scale / max(1, beta)` and adjust by gradient norm? For nonconvex, use `step = step_scale * (c.max()**0.5?)?` Hard.

Wait S=sqrt(2n), uniform cmax=S/n*S? a_i=S/n, cmax=n*(S/n)^2=S^2/n=2. So objective target ~1.5. Gradient of LSE at uniform? beta10, grad magnitude maybe O(S?) ~128. Step ~1/beta*? Let's refine the LP-inspired direction for possible implementation without large LP. We can approximate LP optimum g for current f using iterative thresholding/multiplicative weights in O(n log n)?
LP: max ∑g_j s.t. (f*g)_k≤M, g≥0. Since f fixed, M current max of f*f. This is a feasibility/maximization. We can solve its dual approximately using multiplicative weights? The primal feasible set has polynomial size. We can find g maximizing sum via subgradient? The reciprocal constraints are linear. If we set g_j = exp(-α (F^T?))? Actually to satisfy Fg≤M, we can use water-filling: start g=1, while any c_k>M, decrease g_j in proportion to f_{k-j}. This is like a projection onto polymatroid? There is a known algorithm for maximizing linear objective over {g≥0, Ag≤b} with A nonnegative: it's a linear program; the greedy algorithm for polymatroid if A has nested property, but not here. Could use randomized: for each active lag, reduce g_j where f_{k-j} large. This is similar to gradient descent on dual variables.

Let's derive: We want g small where it contributes to high f*g. f*g = (f * g). If a lag k is high, reducing g_j weighted by f_{k-j} lowers c_k. Accumulate over active k: h_j=∑_{k∈A} λ_k f_{k-j} (which is dual constraint). Then set g_j ∝ 1/h_j (multiplicative). The LP optimal complementarity: g_j>0 only if h_j=1; if h_j>1, g_j=0. This is a heuristic. We can implement:

Then compute g's max convolution with itself? Actually we want use g as candidate; update f ← (1-t)f + t g. The LP ensures f*g≤M roughly, not g*g. But blending may reduce f*f.

Let's detail FISTA gradient functions for convolution with weights.

For LSE gradient:
Given y, c, beta. w = softmax(beta*(c - max(c))) (proportional to exp). We need grad = 2 * convolve(w, reverse? Actually previous `lse_gradient` computes 2*real(ifft(W * conj(A)))[:n], where A=fft(y). Let's verify. c_k =∑_i y_i y_{k-i}. derivative wrt y_j =∑_k w_k 2 y_{k-j}? (where valid). This is 2 ∑_k w_k y_{k-j}. That is cross-correlation. If we FFT y, and W of w, then inverse of W * conj(A) gives ∑ w_k y_{?} maybe. Let's trust previous.
`topk_gradient` similar with w on top idx; `exact_subgradient` average active.

Need compute `c = fft_convolve_self(y)` each iteration. For gradient weights, need fsize = next power of two >= len(w)+n-1. w length 2n-1. Use `fft(w, fsize)` (full) and `fft(y, fsize)` complex. Inverse real.

Can optimize by precomputing FFT of y each iteration? We already compute c = ifft(A*A). We can reuse A for gradient! In run_fista, after computing A? `fft_convolve_self` currently computes A internally. To reuse, modify to return c and A perhaps. But memory okay. We can implement `compute_conv_and_fft(y, fsize)` returns c, A. Use A in gradient. This improves speed.

Let's implement:
```
def convolve_self_and_fft(y):
    n=len(y); size=2*n-1; fsize=1<<(size-1).bit_length()
    A=fft(y, fsize)
    c=np.real(ifft(A*A))
    c = c[:size]
    return c, A, fsize
```
For gradient with weights w length size, compute `W = fft(w, fsize)`, `g=2*real(ifft(W * np.conj(A)))[:n]`.
For topk, create w_full zeros size and set. For active exact, similar.
This saves one FFT of y.

For f*g (LP heuristic) not needed if skip.

Project_simplex optimization:
Could use a faster algorithm without full sort if v doesn't vary much. Let's implement original sort but with local variable arrays. It returns np.maximum(v - theta,0). For sum S, if S <=0 uniform.

Potential issue: y after momentum can have negative; projection clips.

FISTA objective for normalized S: objective = c.max(). But the evaluator objective for returned final may be different if we scale; invariant, so objective same. We'll use `best_obj` as maxconv under S normalization. However when comparing candidates of different n, objective scale? maxconv under S=sqrt(2n) equals evaluator objective. Good.

If candidate n smaller, best_obj may be higher/lower. We should compare `compute_obj_normalized(a)` = 2n maxc / sum^2 (same as evaluator) rather than maxc with S. Use consistent.

In FISTA, we normalize to S, so obj = c.max. For candidate from other length, use `objective(a)` = 2*n*cmax/a.sum()**2.

Potential numerical: c values ~1.5, S~128, a values ~0.016*? No problem. For n=32768, S~256.

Now, implement `run_fista` with time check and reusing FFT.

Pseudo:
```
def run_fista(a0, max_time, rng, beta_init=10.0, beta_rate=0.005, beta_max=3000.0, mode='auto', step_scale=1.0, kick_scale=0.002, patience=2000, k_active=8):
    n=len(a0); S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=np.clip(a0,0,1000).astype(float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: a=np.ones(n)*S/n
    else: a=a*S/s
    y=a.copy(); x=a.copy(); t=1.0
    start=time.monotonic(); best_conv=np.inf; best_a=a.copy(); it=0; last=0; beta=beta_init
    c,A,fsize = convolve_self_and_fft(y)
    best_conv=c.max(); best_a=y.copy()
    while time.monotonic()-start < max_time:
       it+=1
       beta = min(beta_max, beta_init * exp(beta_rate*it))
       cm=c.max()
       if cm < best_conv - 1e-12: update
       stuck = it - last
       # choose grad
       if mode == 'lse' or (mode=='auto' and stuck<...): grad=lse_gradient_fft(y,c,beta,A,fsize,n)
       elif mode == 'topk' or stuck>...: grad=topk_gradient_fft(...)
       else: grad=exact_subgradient_fft(...)
       if not finite or norm==0: break
       step = step_scale / max(1.0,beta)
       # adapt step based on stuck
       if stuck > patience: step *= max(0.2, 1.0 - stuck/(2*patience))
       x_new=project_simplex(y - step*grad, S)
       if not finite: x_new=project_simplex(y, S)
       t_new=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4t*t)); y = x_new + ((t-1)/t_new)*(x_new-x); y=np.maximum(y,0); x=x_new; t=t_new
       # compute new c,A at start of loop (or at end)
       c,A,fsize = convolve_self_and_fft(y)
       if stuck > kick_patience and it%...:
           scale = kick_scale * S / sqrt(n) * (1 + hard?)
           y=project_simplex(best_a + rng.normal(0,scale,n), S)
           x=y.copy(); t=1; beta=beta_init*exp?; c,A,fsize=...
       if it%1000==0: print? maybe throttle.
    return best_a, best_conv
```
Need avoid repeated conv at both start and end; design loop computes at top and updates, then at end compute for next if not loop exit. Simpler compute at top, update, compute at bottom. Ensure time.

`auto` mode logic:
- First 2000 iterations: lse.
- If stuck > 500: use topk with k = min(64, 4+stuck//500)
- If stuck > 5000: use exact subgradient with random active subset of size k maybe 1-16, and noise.
Maybe combine: grad = lse_gradient + 0.5*active_current if stuck.

Use `logging` with counters to avoid print every iter. Print every 2000 or if improvement.

But code will be run maybe many times, prints okay but not too much. We can print every 5s or 2000 iterations.

def consider_candidate(a, current_best):
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<0.01 or len(a)<1: return current_best, np.inf
    obj = objective(a)
    if obj < best_obj[0]: update
```

def time_left(deadline): return deadline - time.monotonic()
```

```

Now, parametric random search implementation top-level:
```
def random_fourier_sequence(n, rng, mean_amp=0.4, max_modes=8, skew=0.0):
    v = np.ones(n)
    x = np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
    if skew !=0:
       v = v + skew * (2*x -1)
    for m in range(1, max_modes+1):
       amp = mean_amp * rng.uniform(0.1, 1.0) / max(1.0, np.sqrt(m))
       phase = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       # choose sin/cos
       if rng.random()<0.5: v += amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*x + phase)
       else: v += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x + phase)
    v = np.maximum(v, 1e-6)
    return normalize_to_s(v, np.sqrt(2*n))
```
But adding cos mode m=1 can create negative; clip distorts. Maybe use `v = base + amp*mode` and if min<0, reduce amp? Could simply generate, clip; FISTA will fix.

Run random search quickly:
```
def quick_random_search(n, time_budget, rng, state):
   deadline=...
   tries=0
   while time<deadline:
      a=random_fourier_sequence(n,rng, mean_amp=rng.uniform(0.1,0.8), max_modes=int(rng.integers(2,8)))
      obj=objective(a)
      update state
      tries++
      if tries%50==0: print
```
For n=4096, each objective FFT 8192 ~0.2ms; 2400 tries/s? Maybe 5000 in 1s. We can do 10k trials in 20s. Keep best random. Then FISTA top candidate.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s - 30.0)
    state = [np.inf, None]

       n_parent=len(parent)

       candidates.append((a_parent, 'parent'))
       # maybe resampled to 8192 if length very different
    else: n_parent=8192; a_parent=cosine initial; candidates.append
    # quick random search at min(4096,n_parent)
    ...
    # Phase 1 quick FISTA on top candidates at original n

Because helper functions top-level, we can manage phases manually.

Maybe structure:
1. Build a list `starts` of (a,label) with up to 6 candidates:
   - parent normalized (if valid)
   - resampled parent to 8192 if parent length !=8192
   - random_fourier at n_parent (or 4096)
   - random_fourier at 4096
   - shifted parent
   - mixed parent/random
2. Quick objective evaluation maybe FISTA 10s each for first 4 to get a warm best.
3. After phase, attempt random search if budget remains (maybe 100s) at n=4096 and n=8192, update.
4. Length sweep/resample of best: Try lengths: [n//2, n*3//4, n, n*5//4, n*2, n*3//2?]. For each, a=resample(best,n); FISTA short 10-20s; update.
5. Intensive FISTA on best with configs for remaining time, split among top 3 configs and kicks.
6. Large n: If best length<32768 and time>300, resample to 16384/32768, FISTA.
7. Final polish: run active mix for remaining 20% on best length.

Detailed schedule example:
- `t0=time.monotonic()`
- Quick starts: allocate 15% (355s) to run up to 5 starts at best length with config0 for ~70s each. Update.
- Random Fourier sampling: allocate 5% (120s) if time, at n=8192 (or 4096) just exact objective (no FISTA) for many. Update best.
- Length sweep: allocate 15% (360s). For ~8 lengths, resample best and run FISTA 45s each (if improvement? just run). Update.
- Main爬山 at best length: allocate 35% (840s). Run a sequence of FISTA configs on best (and maybe best + noise) each 100-200s.
- Large n: allocate 15% (360s). If best length<16384, run at 16384; if<32768 and time, run at 32768. Use resampled best.
- Final: allocate 5% (120s) active polish on overall best length.

But large n FISTA at 32768 360s may be beneficial; if not, main could improve same length. We can adapt: after main, if no improvement in last quick starts, try larger; if improvement, continue larger. Simpler fixed.

Need remember that objective might be lower for larger n; if large n gives worse, keep best.

`run_fista` prints every maybe 30s. Use a global? Top-level print okay.

Let's define `run_fista` signature:
```
def run_fista(a0, max_time, rng, beta_init=10.0, beta_rate=0.004, beta_max=3000.0, mode='auto', step_scale=1.0, max_update=0.2, kick_scale=0.003, patience=3000, print_label=''):
```
Returns `(best_a, best_obj)` where best_obj = evaluator objective for normalized? We'll return best_a and its exact objective (using `objective_from_seq`). In loop, track best_conv under S; at end call `objective_from_seq(best_a)` maybe.

But objective_from_seq rescales if sum not S? best_a sum S. It computes 2n cmax/S^2 = cmax (if S=sqrt(2n)). Good.

Potential issue: If n changes, S changes and objective comparison okay.

Implementation of `run_fista`:
- At start, print label, initial obj.
- Use local `n`, `S`, `size`, maybe fsize compute each c (n fixed).
- Precompute? fsize fixed for n. Use it.
- In loop, compute c,A. To avoid recomputing after kick if no iterations? fine.
- Time check: if `time.monotonic() + 0.5 > deadline` break? Not know deadline; use max_time.
- Need `max_time` ensuring not overrun. Use `start=time.monotonic(); limit=max_time`.
- At print, include `time.monotonic()-start`.

Pseudo code details:
```
def run_fista(...):
    n=len(a0); S=np.sqrt(2*n); a=normalize_to_s(a0,S)
    init_obj=obj_from_seq(a)
    y=a.copy(); x=a.copy(); t=1.0; beta=beta_init; it=0
    best_a=a.copy(); best_obj=init_obj; last_it=0
    c,A,fsize=convolve_self_and_fft(y)
    cm=c.max()
    if cm<best_obj-1e-12: best...
    kick_count=0
    while time.monotonic()-start < max_time:
        it+=1
        beta=min(beta_max, beta_init*np.exp(beta_rate*it))
        # gradient
        use_subg=False
        stuck=it-last_it
        if mode=='lse':
           grad=...
        elif mode=='topk':
           k=...
           grad=...; use_subg=True
        else: # auto
           if stuck < 2000:
              grad=lse
           elif stuck < 8000:
              k=min(256, 16 + stuck//200)
              grad=topk; use_subg=True
           else:
              if rng.random()<0.5: k=max(1,int(stuck//1000)); k=min(512,k); use topk
              else: use lse
              # maybe if very stuck, use topk k=1 with polyak
        gnorm=np.linalg.norm(grad)
        if not finite: break
        step=step_scale/max(1.0,beta)
        if use_subg:
           polyak=max(0.0, cm - 1.0) / max(gnorm*gnorm, 1e-8)
           polyak=min(polyak, 0.1)
           step=max(step, polyak)
        # cap update
        max_upd=max_update
        if stuck>patience:
           max_upd=min(1.0, max_upd*(1.0+stuck/patience*0.5))
        max_st=max(1e-8, max_upd/gnorm)
        step=min(step, max_st)
        # if no improvement a lot, reduce step? maybe
        x_new=project_simplex(y - step*grad, S)
        if not all finite: x_new=project_simplex(y,S)
        t_new=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t*t))
        y=x_new + ((t-1)/t_new)*(x_new-x)
        y=np.maximum(y,0)
        s=y.sum()
        if s<1e-12: y=a.copy(); x=y.copy(); t=1
        else: y=y*S/s
        x=x_new
        t=t_new
        c,A,fsize=convolve_self_and_fft(y)
        cm=c.max()
        if cm < best_obj - 1e-12:
            best_obj=cm; best_a=y.copy(); last_it=it
        if it % 10000 ==0:
            t=1
            # small periodic noise
        if stuck > patience and (it % 500 ==0 or rng.random()<0.01):
            kick_scale_eff = kick_scale * (1.0 + min(5.0, stuck/patience))
            noise = rng.normal(0.0, kick_scale_eff * S / np.sqrt(n), n)
            y = project_simplex(best_a + noise, S)
            x = y.copy(); t=1.0; beta=beta_init
            kick_count+=1
            # recompute c,A
            c,A,fsize=convolve_self_and_fft(y)
            cm=c.max()
        if it % 2000 ==0: print
    # final: maybe run one more exact objective
    obj_eff=objective_from_seq(best_a)
    if obj_eff < best_obj: best_obj=obj_eff
    return best_a, best_obj
```
Need be careful: `best_obj` updated with cm under normalized S, but after y projected/renormalized, objective = cm. Good.

Momentum y may not equal x if projection; okay.

The gradient from LSE uses `y` (current extrapolated point) but objective evaluated at y. FISTA standard should evaluate at y. We compute gradient at y. Good.

Now, objective_from_seq for a with arbitrary sum:
```
def objective_from_seq(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<0.01 or len(a)<1: return inf
    c,_,_=convolve_self_and_fft(a) # uses a not normalized, maybe huge if >1000? a clipped
    return float(2*len(a)*c.max()/(s*s))
```
This computes FFT of unnormalized a possibly large (if parent values >1000, clipped). Values after clipping up to1000, sum huge; c could be ~1e9, sum^2 ~1e13, fine. But for random Fourier normalized S, values ~0.016.

However `convolve_self_and_fft` returns c and A; for objective, no need A. But okay. Could use `fft_convolve_self` to avoid A? Reuse.

Could there be overflow for large n if a values scaled to1000 and n=32768: sum ~16M, sum^2~2.5e14, conv max ~? 1e12, double fine.

But if objective_from_seq called on a normalized S with sum~256, no issue.

```
def run_random_search_and_polish(state, n, sample_budget, polish_budget, rng):
   local=[np.inf,None]
   deadline= time.monotonic()+sample_budget
   count=0
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
      a = random_fourier_sequence(n,rng,...)
      obj=objective_from_seq(a)
      if obj<local[0]: local[0]=obj; local[1]=a
      count+=1
      if count%5000==0: print
   update_state(state, local[1])
   if polish_budget>10 and local[1] is not None:
      a_best,obj=run_fista(local[1], polish_budget, rng, label='rand-polish', **cfg_rand)
      update_state(state,a_best)
```

We can precompute a dictionary of basis vectors for frequencies 1..max_modes at length n. Memory: max_modes=12, 2 sin/cos each length 8192 => 24*8192*8=1.5MB, fine. Build once:
```
def build_fourier_basis(n, max_modes):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
    cos_list=[]; sin_list=[]
    for m in range(1,max_modes+1):
       ang=2*np.pi*m*x
       cos_list.append(np.cos(ang)); sin_list.append(np.sin(ang))
    return cos_list, sin_list
```
Then random sequence:
```
def random_fourier_sequence_basis(n, rng, cos_list, sin_list, mean_amp, max_modes, skew):
   v=np.ones(n)
   if skew!=0: v = v + skew * np.linspace(-1,1,n) # precompute skew vector
   for m in range(1,min(max_modes,len(cos_list))+1):
      amp = mean_amp * rng.uniform(0.1,1.0) / max(1.0,np.sqrt(m))
      phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); c=cos(phase); s=sin(phase)
      v += amp*(c*cos_list[m-1] + s*sin_list[m-1])
   np.maximum(v, 1e-4, out=v)
   return normalize_to_s(v, sqrt(2n))
```
Also precompute `skew_v = np.linspace(-1,1,n)`.
`normalize_to_s` uses sum and clip; O(n). Good.

Let's write `update_state(state, a)` carefully:
```
def update_state(state, a):
    if a is None: return state
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if len(a)==0: return state
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12: return state
    cmax = convolve_self_and_fft(a)[0].max() # expensive
    if not np.isfinite(cmax): return state
    obj = float(2.0 * len(a) * cmax / (s*s))
    if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < state[0] - 1e-12:
        state[0]=obj; state[1]=a.copy()
    return state
```
This recomputes FFT; for parent many times maybe fine. We can have a version `update_state_obj(state, a, obj)` to avoid recompute when we know. For FISTA returns obj under normalized S. `project_simplex` with S maybe large (256). Original algorithm:
```
def project_simplex(v, S):
    n=len(v)
    if S<=0: return np.zeros(n)
    w=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cumsum=np.cumsum(w)
    idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float)
    tc=(cumsum-S)/idx
    valid=tc <= w
    rho=int(np.max(np.where(valid)[0])+1) if np.any(valid) else n
    theta=(cumsum[rho-1]-S)/rho
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
If S > sum(v)? It projects to sum less than S? The projection onto sum=S for v with sum<S? The formula assumes S is sum target and works if sum(v)>=S? If sum(v)<S, theta negative? Let's check: If S=10, v all0, cumsum0, tc=0/idx=0 <=0 valid all, rho=n, theta=0, returns0 sum0 not S. Projection onto {x>=0,sum=S} when sum v<S is not unique? It should add uniformly to reach S; simple formula with nonnegative and sum target can handle by theta negative: Need maximize? Actually projection of v onto simplex sum=S with S>sum(v) includes adding mass; solution x_i = max(v_i - θ,0) with θ could be negative, but if all v_i -θ>0 for some θ<0, sum = sum v -nθ =S -> θ=(sum v -S)/n negative. The water-filling formula with sorted w and condition works if we allow theta=(cumsum[rho-1]-S)/rho; for n active, theta=(0-S)/n=-S/n; returns v+ S/n, sum S. Condition valid? tc=(cumsum-S)/idx = -S/idx; w=0; valid tc<=w? -small <=0 true for all, rho=n, theta negative. Good. Our code does that: valid true, theta negative. `np.maximum(v-theta,0)` adds. Good.

If S too small? okay.

Now, final scaling: If best sequence normalized sum S with max small, scaling to max 999 gives sum ~999*S/max. If max too small (e.g., sequence all zeros? no), sum maybe >0.01. If max >0.999? no.

Now, possible performance bottlenecks:
- `project_simplex` sorting each iteration. For n=32768, sort 65k? Wait v length n=32768. okay.
- `np.exp` over 2n each iteration. For n=32768, 65535 exp ~0.3ms.
- FFT size 65536 (since 2n-1=65535, next pow2 65536), good. Convolution one FFT + ifft for c; gradient one FFT for weights + ifft. Total 2 FFT forward? c uses A fsize (one forward? fft(A?) Actually `fft(y)` one forward, ifft(A*A) one inverse. Gradient: fft(w) forward, ifft inverse. Total 2 forward +2 inverse per iter? Could be ~4 transforms. ```
def random_fourier_sequence(n, rng, mean_amp=None, max_modes=8, skew_range=0.4):

```
Better in random search, build basis once and pass. We'll implement `run_random_search_and_polish` builds basis.

```
def random_fourier_from_basis(n, rng, cos_list, sin_list, skew_vec, base_log=None):
    v = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    skew = rng.uniform(-0.4,0.4)
    v += skew * skew_vec
    max_m = min(len(cos_list), int(rng.integers(1, 9)))
    mean_amp = rng.uniform(0.05,0.45)
    for m in range(1, max_m+1):
       amp = mean_amp * rng.uniform(0.2, 1.0) / np.sqrt(float(m))
       phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi)
       v += amp * (np.cos(phase)*cos_list[m-1] + np.sin(phase)*sin_list[m-1])
    np.maximum(v, 1e-4, out=v)
    return normalize_to_s(v, np.sqrt(2.0*n))
```
`np.sqrt` per sample okay. Could pass S.

`run_random_search_and_polish`:
```
def run_random_search_and_polish(state, n, sample_budget, polish_budget, rng):
    if sample_budget <=0: return
    max_modes=8
    cos_list,sin_list,skew_vec=build_fourier_basis(n,max_modes)
    S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    local=[np.inf,None]
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+sample_budget; count=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       a=random_fourier_from_basis(n,rng,cos_list,sin_list,skew_vec,S)
       obj=objective_from_seq(a)
       if obj < local[0]: local[0]=obj; local[1]=a.copy()
       count+=1
       if count % 2000 == 0: print
    if local[1] is not None:
       update_state_obj(state, local[1], local[0])
       if polish_budget >15:
          a_best,obj=run_fista(local[1], polish_budget, rng, print_label='rand', beta_init=10,beta_rate=0.002,beta_max=5000,mode='auto',step_scale=1.0,max_update=0.3,kick_scale=0.003,patience=3000)
          update_state_obj(state,a_best,obj)
```

Now, `add_noise_sequence` top-level:
```
def add_noise_sequence(a, rng, sigma):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).copy(); n=len(a); S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000); s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: a=np.ones(n)*S/n
    else: a=a*S/s
    noise=rng.normal(0.0, sigma * S / np.sqrt(n), n)
    a=np.maximum(a+noise,0.0)
    return normalize_to_s(a,S)
```
If sigma is fraction of S/sqrt(n), e.g., 0.01 gives coordinate noise 0.01*sqrt(2) ~0.014 for n=8192, relative uniform 90%? Wait S/sqrt(n)=sqrt(2) ~1.414; sigma0.01 =>0.014, uniform coordinate S/n=0.0156. So 90% noise. Too large. Use sigma 0.001 for 9%. For FISTA kick, kick_scale=0.003 * S/sqrt(n) =0.004, 25%. Good. Function:
```
def softmax_lse_objective(theta, n, beta):
    p = softmax(theta)
    a = np.sqrt(2.0*n)*p
    c, A, fsize = convolve_self_and_fft(a)
    cm=c.max(); shifted=beta*(c-cm); shifted-=shifted.max(); e=np.exp(shifted); val=cm + np.log(e.sum())/beta
    return val
```
Gradient:
```
def softmax_lse_gradient(theta, n, beta):
    p=softmax; a=S*p; c,A,fsize=...; compute softmax weights w; g=grad_from_weights(w,A,fsize,n); grad_theta = a*(g - np.dot(a,g)/S)
    return grad_theta
```

Let's think about using active-set LP heuristic as a different method that may escape. We can implement `run_lp_heuristic` with time control and perhaps include one run. It may be useful.

Detailed LP heuristic:
- Input a normalized S. `f=a`. `g=a.copy()`.
- M = max(f*f) maybe current objective. We want update f.
- Iterate:
  c_fg = convolve_cross(f,g) (linear). max_fg = c_fg.max()
  We can scale g such that c_fg max <= M? If g sum not fixed. We can use `alpha = M / max_fg` and scale g *= alpha (if alpha<1? if max_fg >M decrease; if <M increase? But sum changes. The state method LP max sum, then rescale. For blend, keep sum S.)
  Active weights w from c_fg (softmax beta or top k). Compute h = cross_correlation(w, f): h_j = ∑_k w_k f_{k-j}. This is `gradient_from_weights(w, A_f, fsize)`? We need A_f of f. Since h = ∑ w_k f_{k-j}. `gradient_from_weights(w, A_f, fsize, n)` returns 2h? It returns 2∑ w_k f_{k-j}. We can halve.
  Update g = exp(-eta * h / f_scale) or `g = 1/(1+eta*h)`. Normalize g to S.
  candidate = (1-trend) f + t*g; t small (e.g., 0.2) decreasing.
  If objective(candidate) < objective(f): f=candidate; M = max(f*f); else reduce t.
  Periodically evaluate.
This is O(2 cross conv + one self) per iter. Could be slower than FISTA but maybe different.

We need a cross-convolution function:
```
def convolve_cross_and_fft(a,b):
   size=2n-1? if both length n; fsize next pow2; A=fft(a,fsize); B=fft(b,fsize); c=real(ifft(A*B))[:size]
```
For h weights w length 2n-1 and f length n: need fsize >= len(w)+n-1 = 4n-2? Wait cross-correlation h_j=∑_k w_k f_{k-j}. This is convolution of w and reversed f? Let's derive using gradient_from_weights: It assumed c_k=a_i a_{k-i}, gradient =2∑_k w_k a_{k-i}. It used `ifft(W * conj(A))` with fsize >= len(w)+n-1? For w length 2n-1, a length n, linear cross-correlation length? `W*conj(A)` inverse gives circular correlation. Need fsize at least len(w)+n-1? Previous `lse_gradient` used fsize = 1 << (len(w)+n-1).bit_length(), not the same as self-conv fsize (2n). len(w)=2n-1, plus n-1 => 3n-2, next pow maybe 4n for n power of two? For n=8192, self fsize 16384, gradient fsize next pow (24575?) =32768. Previous code used `fsize = 1 << (len(w) + n - 1).bit_length()` indeed larger. I noted earlier. Need implement gradient with appropriate fsize. This increases cost (transform size 32768 for n=8192). Previous did that. For FISTA, self fsize=16384; gradient fsize=32768. That's okay. We must not reuse self fsize for gradient; our `convolve_self_and_fft` returns A with fsize self. Gradient needs larger fsize? Wait formula `ifft(W * conj(A))[:n]` with circular convolution length fsize. If fsize >= len(w)+n-1? Let's test: w length 2n-1, a length n. We want h_i=∑_{k} w_k a_{k-i}. This is cross-correlation. The linear result length (2n-1)+n-1? Actually w index k 0..2n-2, a index j 0..n-1, need k-i in [0,n-1]. i 0..n-1. The sum over k. This can be seen as convolution of w_rev? The output length maybe 3n-2. If fsize >= 3n-2, inverse gives linear. If fsize = self fsize =2n (for n power2?), for n=8192 self fsize=16384, 3n-2=24574 >16384, so gradient wrong! Previous code used larger fsize. Important. We must create gradient FFT with `gfsize = next_pow2(len(w)+n-1)`. That means for n=8192, gfsize=32768, two extra transforms. Still okay. For n=32768, gfsize=131072 (since 3n-2=98k, next pow 131072). That's bigger; cost higher. But FISTA at 32768 with gradient size 131072 maybe 5-10ms per iter, 1000 iter 10s, okay.

Our `convolve_self_and_fft` returns A with self fsize 65536 for n=32768. Gradient needs 131072. We cannot reuse A directly because different length. We could compute gradient from A by zero-padding? The DFT at larger length not same. Need compute `A_g = fft(y, gfsize)` each iteration. That adds a forward transform of y at gfsize. Or we can compute gradient using `fft(y, gfsize)`. So per iteration: self conv may use fsize1; gradient uses fsize2 transforms. We can choose to use the same fsize for both? If we set self fsize to gfsize (next pow2 >=3n-2), then c = ifft(A*A)[:2n-1] is also correct (zero-padded longer). Then we can reuse A for gradient with same fsize! This simplifies and avoids computing A at two lengths. The self convolution with fsize=32768 for n=8192 instead of16384 doubles self cost but can reuse. Previous separate maybe more efficient? Let's compare: Self at16384 (F+IFFT) + gradient at32768 (F+IFFT) = transforms sizes 16+F32+I32 etc. Using 32768 for all: F32 + I32 (self) + F32w + I32 (grad) = one extra F32 for y vs previous, but saves? Previous had F16+I16+F32+I32. Which is cheaper? For power-of-two, transform cost ~N log N. Previous: 16*14 +32*15 =224+480=706 units; new: 32*15*2? Actually new: self F32+I32 (2*480), grad Fw32+I32 (2*480) total 1920. Previous: F16+I16 (2*224=448) + F32y + I32g (2*480=960) total1408. Previous 25% cheaper. But code complexity. We can cache if multiple gradient computations per iter? LSE one. Topk uses same A_g. So per iteration: self fsize1 transforms + gradient fsize2 transforms (A_g forward + W forward + ifft). Good.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    try: budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except: budget_s = 2400.0
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s - 30.0)
    state = [np.inf, None]
    parent = get_parent_sequence()
    if parent is None: parent = default_sequence(8192)
    update_state(state, parent)
    if state[1] is None:
       state[1] = default_sequence(8192); update_state(state,state[1])
    n_main = len(state[1])
    if n_main > 24576:
       a_r = resample_sequence(state[1], 16384)
       update_state(state, a_r)
    # If current best length <2048 maybe resample to 4096?

       a_r = resample_sequence(state[1], 4096)

    print initial

def convolve_self(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    size=2*n-1
    fsize=1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A=fft(a, fsize)
    c=np.real(ifft(A*A))[:size]
    # remove tiny imaginary? real already
    return c
```

Step computation:
```
is_subg = (grad_source == 'topk')
if is_subg:
   base = step_scale / (1.0 + 0.0002*it)
   polyak = max(0.0, cm - 1.0) / max(gnorm*gnorm, 1e-8)
   polyak = min(polyak, 0.2)
   base = max(base, polyak)
else:
   base = step_scale / max(1.0, beta)
step=base
max_upd = max_update
if stuck > patience:
   max_upd = min(0.8, max_upd * (1.0 + stuck/(2.0*patience)))
step = min(step, max_upd / max(gnorm,1e-12))
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing 2*n*max(a*a)/(sum(a)^2).

Strategy:
- Use the previous best sequence if available.
- Normalize to sum sqrt(2n), so the objective is exactly max convolution.
- Run accelerated projected subgradient/FISTA variants for the smoothed
  max-convolution, with adaptive LSE smoothing, top-lag subgradients,
  periodic random kicks, and multi-scale length changes.
- Include random smooth Fourier starts to avoid local minima.
- Finally return a safely scaled list (max <= 990) whose value is finite.
"""
import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.fft import fft, ifft


CFG_QUICK = dict(
    beta_init=5.0,
    beta_rate=0.002,
    beta_max=3000.0,
    mode="auto",
    step_scale=1.0,
    max_update=0.30,
    kick_scale=0.003,
    patience=3000,
    topk_init=16,
    topk_max=512,
)

CFG_MID = dict(
    beta_init=20.0,
    beta_rate=0.001,
    beta_max=10000.0,
    mode="auto",
    step_scale=0.80,
    max_update=0.20,
    kick_scale=0.002,
    patience=5000,
    topk_init=8,
    topk_max=256,
)

CFG_POLISH = dict(
    beta_init=80.0,
    beta_rate=0.0005,
    beta_max=50000.0,
    mode="auto",
    step_scale=0.50,
    max_update=0.12,
    kick_scale=0.001,
    patience=8000,
    topk_init=4,
    topk_max=128,
)

CFG_SUBG = dict(
    beta_init=1.0,
    beta_rate=0.0,
    beta_max=1.0,
    mode="topk",
    step_scale=0.18,
    max_update=0.18,
    kick_scale=0.002,
    patience=4500,
    topk_init=8,
    topk_max=256,
)


def seconds_left(deadline):
    return max(0.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic())


def next_power_of_two(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def self_fft_size(n):
    return next_power_of_two(2 * int(n) - 1)


def grad_fft_size(n):
    # w has length 2n-1, y has length n.
    return next_power_of_two(3 * int(n) - 2)


def normalize_to_s(v, S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = len(v)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-15:
        v = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(n)
    return v * (float(S) / s)


def convolve_self(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    fsize = self_fft_size(n)
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    c = np.real(ifft(A * A))[:size]
    # Remove tiny negative numerical noise.
    c[np.abs(c) < 1e-14] = 0.0
    return c


def objective_from_seq(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    c = convolve_self(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * len(a) * cmax / (s * s))


def update_state(state, a):
    if a is None:
        return state
    obj = objective_from_seq(a)
    return update_state_obj(state, a, obj)


def update_state_obj(state, a, obj):
    if a is None:
        return state
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return state
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.isfinite(obj):
        return state
    if obj < state[0] - 1e-14:
        state[0] = float(obj)
        state[1] = a.copy()
    elif state[1] is None:
        state[0] = float(obj)
        state[1] = a.copy()
    return state


def softmax_beta(c, beta):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    size = c.size
    if size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    cm = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cm):
        return np.ones(size, dtype=np.float64) / size
    shifted = float(beta) * (c - cm)
    shifted_max = float(np.max(shifted))
    shifted = shifted - shifted_max
    e = np.exp(shifted)
    tot = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(tot) or tot < 1e-300:
        return np.ones(size, dtype=np.float64) / size
    return e / tot


def topk_weights(c, k):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    size = c.size
    k = int(k)
    if size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if k <= 0:
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=np.int64)
        w = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w
    if k >= size:
        return np.ones(size, dtype=np.float64) / size
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
    w = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = 1.0 / k
    return w


def gradient_from_weights(y, w, A, gfsize, n):
    # A must be fft(y, gfsize). Returns 2 * sum_k w_k y_{k-i}, zero-padded.
    W = fft(w, gfsize)
    g = 2.0 * np.real(ifft(W * np.conj(A)))[:n]
    return g


def project_simplex(v, S):
    n = len(v)
    S = float(S)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if S <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    w = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cumsum = np.cumsum(w)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    tc = (cumsum - S) / idx
    valid = tc <= w
    if np.any(valid):
        rho = int(np.max(np.where(valid)[0]) + 1)
    else:
        rho = n
    theta = float((cumsum[rho - 1] - S) / rho)
    out = v - theta
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def run_fista(a0, max_time, rng,
              beta_init=10.0, beta_rate=0.003, beta_max=5000.0,
              mode="auto", step_scale=1.0, max_update=0.20,
              kick_scale=0.003, patience=4000,
              topk_init=16, topk_max=512,
              print_label="fista"):
    a0 = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a0.size)
    if n <= 0 or max_time <= 0.0:
        return a0, objective_from_seq(a0)

    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = normalize_to_s(a0, S)
    best_obj = float(objective_from_seq(a))
    best_a = a.copy()

    x = a.copy()
    y = a.copy()
    t_mom = 1.0
    it = 0
    beta_it = 0
    last_improve = 0
    last_kick = 0
    start = time.monotonic()
    limit = float(max_time)
    last_print = 0.0

    self_size = self_fft_size(n)
    grad_size = grad_fft_size(n)

    print("  [%s] start n=%d obj=%.7f budget=%.1fs" % (print_label, n, best_obj, max_time))

    while True:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        if elapsed >= limit:
            break

        it += 1
        if mode != "topk":
            beta_it += 1
            beta = min(float(beta_max), float(beta_init) * np.exp(float(beta_rate) * float(beta_it)))
        else:
            beta = 1.0

        c = convolve_self(y)
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        cm = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(cm):
            break

        if cm < best_obj - 1e-12:
            best_obj = cm
            best_a = y.copy()
            last_improve = it

        stuck = it - last_improve
        A_g = fft(y, grad_size)

        if mode == "lse":
            use_subg = False
            w = softmax_beta(c, beta)
        elif mode == "topk":
            use_subg = True
            k = int(topk_init) + it // 500
            if k < 1:
                k = 1
            if k > int(topk_max):
                k = int(topk_max)
            w = topk_weights(c, k)
        else:
            # adaptive: smooth LSE first, then active-lag subgradients.
            if stuck < 2500:
                use_subg = False
                w = softmax_beta(c, beta)
            else:
                use_subg = True
                k = int(topk_init)
                if stuck > 5000:
                    k += (stuck - 5000) // 120
                if stuck > 20000 and rng.random() < 0.45:
                    k = 1
                if k < 1:
                    k = 1
                if k > int(topk_max):
                    k = int(topk_max)
                w = topk_weights(c, k)

        grad = gradient_from_weights(y, w, A_g, grad_size, n)
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if not np.isfinite(gnorm) or gnorm < 1e-14:
            # Stationary or broken. Either kick or stop.
            if it - last_kick > patience:
                sigma = float(kick_scale) * S / np.sqrt(float(n))
                noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
                y = project_simplex(best_a + noise, S)
                x = y.copy()
                t_mom = 1.0
                beta_it = 0
                last_kick = it
                continue
            break

        if use_subg:
            base_step = float(step_scale) / (1.0 + 0.0002 * float(it))
            polyak = max(0.0, cm - 1.0) / max(gnorm * gnorm, 1e-8)
            polyak = min(polyak, 0.25)
            base_step = max(base_step, polyak)
        else:
            base_step = float(step_scale) / max(1.0, float(beta))

        max_upd = float(max_update)
        if stuck > int(patience):
            max_upd = min(0.85, max_upd * (1.0 + float(stuck) / (2.0 * float(patience))))

        step = base_step
        cap = max_upd / max(gnorm, 1e-12)
        if step > cap:
            step = cap
        if step <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(step):
            step = 1e-5

        x_new = project_simplex(y - step * grad, S)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x_new)):
            x_new = project_simplex(y, S)
            y = x_new
            x = x_new
            t_mom = 1.0
            last_kick = it
            continue

        t_new = 0.5 * (1.0 + np.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0 * t_mom * t_mom))
        y = x_new + ((t_mom - 1.0) / t_new) * (x_new - x)
        y = np.maximum(y, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(y))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12:
            y = best_a.copy()
            x = y.copy()
            t_mom = 1.0
            beta_it = 0
            last_kick = it
        else:
            y = y * (S / s)
        x = x_new
        t_mom = t_new

        # Periodic momentum reset with tiny noise.
        if it % 10000 == 0:
            t_mom = 1.0
            sig = max(1e-5, 0.0008 * S / np.sqrt(float(n)))
            y = project_simplex(y + rng.normal(0.0, sig, n), S)
            x = y.copy()

        # Hard re-start from best when stuck.
        if stuck > int(patience) and (it - last_kick) > 500:
            boost = 1.0 + min(5.0, float(stuck) / float(patience))
            sigma = float(kick_scale) * boost * S / np.sqrt(float(n))
            if sigma <= 0.0:
                sigma = 1e-5
            y = project_simplex(best_a + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n), S)
            x = y.copy()
            t_mom = 1.0
            beta_it = 0
            last_kick = it

        if it % 2000 == 0:
            now = time.monotonic() - start
            if now - last_print > 20.0:
                print("    [%s] t=%4d it=%6d obj=%.7f beta=%.1f" % (
                    print_label, int(now), it, best_obj, beta))
                last_print = now

    final_obj = float(objective_from_seq(best_a))
    if final_obj < best_obj:
        best_obj = final_obj
    print("  [%s] done obj=%.7f time=%.1fs" % (print_label, best_obj, time.monotonic() - start))
    return best_a, best_obj


def default_sequence(n):
    n = int(max(64, n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    v = 0.54 + 0.46 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
    v = np.maximum(v, 1e-6)
    return normalize_to_s(v, np.sqrt(2.0 * n))


def fallback_sequence(n=8192):
    return default_sequence(int(n))


def get_parent_sequence():
    try:
        a = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
        return None
    return normalize_to_s(a, np.sqrt(2.0 * a.size))


def resample_sequence(a, n_new):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n_new = int(n_new)
    if a.size == 0 or n_new <= 0:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.size == n_new:
        return normalize_to_s(a, np.sqrt(2.0 * n_new))
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_new, endpoint=False)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return normalize_to_s(out, np.sqrt(2.0 * n_new))


def add_noise_sequence(a, rng, sigma):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return None
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = normalize_to_s(a, S)
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma) * S / np.sqrt(float(n)), n)
    a = np.maximum(a + noise, 0.0)
    return normalize_to_s(a, S)


def build_fourier_basis(n, max_modes):
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    cos_list = []
    sin_list = []
    for m in range(1, max_modes + 1):
        ang = 2.0 * np.pi * float(m) * x
        cos_list.append(np.cos(ang))
        sin_list.append(np.sin(ang))
    skew_vec = 2.0 * x - 1.0
    return cos_list, sin_list, skew_vec


def random_fourier_from_basis(n, rng, cos_list, sin_list, skew_vec, S):
    v = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    skew = rng.uniform(-0.45, 0.45)
    v += skew * skew_vec
    max_m = int(min(len(cos_list), rng.integers(1, 9)))
    mean_amp = rng.uniform(0.05, 0.42)
    for m in range(max_m):
        amp = mean_amp * rng.uniform(0.20, 1.00) / np.sqrt(float(m + 1.0))
        phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        v += amp * (np.cos(phase) * cos_list[m] + np.sin(phase) * sin_list[m])
    v = np.maximum(v, 1e-4)
    return normalize_to_s(v, S)


def run_random_search_and_polish(state, n, sample_budget, polish_budget, rng):
    n = int(n)
    if n < 256 or sample_budget <= 0.0:
        return
    if n > 16384:
        n = 16384
    max_modes = 8
    cos_list, sin_list, skew_vec = build_fourier_basis(n, max_modes)
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    local_state = [np.inf, None]
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(sample_budget)
    count = 0
    print("  random search n=%d budget=%.1fs" % (n, sample_budget))
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        a = random_fourier_from_basis(n, rng, cos_list, sin_list, skew_vec, S)
        obj = objective_from_seq(a)
        if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < local_state[0] - 1e-14:
            local_state[0] = float(obj)
            local_state[1] = a.copy()
        count += 1
        if count % 3000 == 0:
            print("    random samples=%d best=%.7f t=%.1fs" % (count, local_state[0], time.monotonic() - start))

    if local_state[1] is not None:
        print("  random best before polish=%.7f" % local_state[0])
        update_state_obj(state, local_state[1], local_state[0])
        if polish_budget > 15.0:
            a_best, obj_best = run_fista(
                local_state[1],
                polish_budget,
                rng,
                print_label="rand-polish",
                **CFG_QUICK
            )
            update_state_obj(state, a_best, obj_best)
    else:
        print("  random search produced no usable sequence")


def make_final_list(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        a = fallback_sequence(8192)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    m = float(np.max(a)) if a.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        a = fallback_sequence(8192)
        m = float(np.max(a))
    scale = 990.0 / m
    a = a * scale
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
        # Very unlikely, but ensure evaluator acceptance.
        a = fallback_sequence(8192)
        m = float(np.max(a))
        a = np.clip(a * (990.0 / m), 0.0, 1000.0)
    return [float(v) for v in a]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 42
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or budget_s <= 0.0:
        budget_s = 2400.0

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s - 30.0)
    state = [np.inf, None]

    parent = get_parent_sequence()
    if parent is None:
        parent = default_sequence(8192)
    update_state(state, parent)

    if state[1] is None:
        state[1] = default_sequence(8192)
        update_state(state, state[1])

    print("initial best obj=%.8f n=%d" % (state[0], len(state[1])))

    # If parent is much longer than manageable, resample one version too.
    if len(state[1]) > 24576:
        a_r = resample_sequence(state[1], 16384)
        update_state(state, a_r)

    # If the current best is tiny, try a moderately sized version.
    if len(state[1]) < 2048:
        a_r = resample_sequence(state[1], 4096)
        update_state(state, a_r)

    # Quick warmup on the initial best.
    if seconds_left(deadline) > 60.0 and state[1] is not None:
        b = min(180.0, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.12)
        if b > 10.0:
            ab, ob = run_fista(state[1], b, rng, print_label="quick-parent", **CFG_QUICK)
            update_state_obj(state, ab, ob)
            print("after quick warmup obj=%.8f n=%d" % (state[0], len(state[1])))

    # Random smooth Fourier search from scratch.
    if seconds_left(deadline) > 260.0:
        n_rand = 4096
        sample_b = min(90.0, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.045)
        polish_b = min(150.0, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.065)
        if sample_b > 10.0 and polish_b > 15.0:
            run_random_search_and_polish(state, n_rand, sample_b, polish_b, rng)
            print("after random search obj=%.8f n=%d" % (state[0], len(state[1])))

    # Multi-length sweep around the current best, plus a few canonical lengths.
    if seconds_left(deadline) > 280.0 and state[1] is not None:
        n_best = int(len(state[1]))
        lengths = []
        for f in [0.75, 0.875, 1.0, 1.125, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0, 0.5]:
            n = int(round(n_best * f))
            if 1024 <= n <= 32768:
                lengths.append(n)
        for n in [2048, 4096, 8192, 16384, 32768]:
            if n not in lengths:
                lengths.append(n)
        lengths = sorted(set(lengths))
        total = seconds_left(deadline) * 0.16
        per = max(10.0, total / max(1, len(lengths)))
        print("length sweep: %s" % str(lengths))
        for n in lengths:
            if seconds_left(deadline) < 25.0:
                break
            if n == len(state[1]):
                a_start = add_noise_sequence(state[1], rng, 0.0015)
            else:
                a_start = resample_sequence(state[1], n)
            if a_start is None:
                continue
            b = min(per, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.50)
            if b < 8.0:
                continue
            ab, ob = run_fista(a_start, b, rng, print_label="len%d" % n, **CFG_QUICK)
            update_state_obj(state, ab, ob)

    # Main iterative work on several configurations.
    if seconds_left(deadline) > 200.0 and state[1] is not None:
        configs = [
            (CFG_MID, "mid", 0.0),
            (CFG_POLISH, "high-beta", 0.0010),
            (CFG_SUBG, "subgrad", 0.0018),
        ]
        for cfg, lab, sig in configs:
            if seconds_left(deadline) < 45.0:
                break
            b = min(260.0, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.16)
            if b < 15.0:
                continue
            if sig > 0.0:
                base = add_noise_sequence(state[1], rng, sig)
            else:
                base = state[1].copy()
            ab, ob = run_fista(base, b, rng, print_label=lab, **cfg)
            update_state_obj(state, ab, ob)
            print("after %s obj=%.8f n=%d" % (lab, state[0], len(state[1])))

    # Larger length phase, if time permits.
    if seconds_left(deadline) > 300.0 and state[1] is not None:
        n_cur = int(len(state[1]))
        if n_cur < 16384:
            a_big = resample_sequence(state[1], 16384)
            b = min(300.0, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.16)
            if a_big is not None and b > 20.0:
                ab, ob = run_fista(a_big, b, rng, print_label="n16384", **CFG_MID)
                update_state_obj(state, ab, ob)
                print("after n16384 obj=%.8f n=%d" % (state[0], len(state[1])))

        if seconds_left(deadline) > 260.0:
            n_cur = int(len(state[1]))
            if n_cur < 32768:
                a_big = resample_sequence(state[1], 32768)
                b = min(300.0, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.16)
                if a_big is not None and b > 20.0:
                    ab, ob = run_fista(a_big, b, rng, print_label="n32768", **CFG_MID)
                    update_state_obj(state, ab, ob)
                    print("after n32768 obj=%.8f n=%d" % (state[0], len(state[1])))

    # Final polish.
    if seconds_left(deadline) > 30.0 and state[1] is not None:
        b = min(220.0, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.75)
        if b > 10.0:
            ab, ob = run_fista(state[1], b, rng, print_label="final", **CFG_POLISH)
            update_state_obj(state, ab, ob)

        if seconds_left(deadline) > 20.0 and state[1] is not None:
            b2 = min(120.0, seconds_left(deadline) * 0.75)
            if b2 > 10.0:
                ab2, ob2 = run_fista(state[1], b2, rng, print_label="final-subg", **CFG_SUBG)
                update_state_obj(state, ab2, ob2)

    if state[1] is None:
        final = make_final_list(fallback_sequence(8192))
    else:
        final = make_final_list(state[1])

    try:
        ver = float(evaluate_sequence(final))
    except Exception:
        ver = np.inf

    if np.isfinite(ver):
        print("FINAL verified objective = %.8f, n=%d" % (ver, len(final)))
    else:
        print("WARNING: final evaluate_sequence returned inf, using fallback")
        final = make_final_list(fallback_sequence(8192))
        try:
            ver2 = float(evaluate_sequence(final))
            print("fallback verified objective = %.8f" % ver2)
        except Exception:
            pass

    return final
```
